Chain, Implicit, and Inverse | Sections 3.1-3.5 | Canonical destination-first source

1. Notes Example / You Try It

Section 3.1 - Chain Rule

U3-S1-NOTE-EX01
Section 3.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

For $y=8 \left(x - 1\right)^{3}$, identify an inner function and an outer function, then state the factor contributed by differentiating the inner function.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: Inner: $2 x - 2$; outer: $u^3$; inner-derivative factor: $2$.
Solution / scoring notes: Trace $x\to 2 x - 2\to (2 x - 2)^3$; the chain rule multiplies by $2$.
U3-S1-NOTE-YTI01
Section 3.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

For $y=\left(3 x + 5\right)^{4}$, identify an inner function and an outer function, then state the factor contributed by differentiating the inner function.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: Inner: $- 3 x - 5$; outer: $u^4$; inner-derivative factor: $-3$.
Solution / scoring notes: Trace $x\to - 3 x - 5\to (- 3 x - 5)^4$; the chain rule multiplies by $-3$.
U3-S1-NOTE-EX02
Section 3.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Differentiate $f(x)=81 \left(x + 1\right)^{4}$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $f'(x)=324 \left(x + 1\right)^{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: Power the outside, keep the inside, then multiply by $3$: $324 \left(x + 1\right)^{3}$.
U3-S1-NOTE-YTI02
Section 3.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Differentiate $f(x)=\left(2 x - 5\right)^{3}$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $f'(x)=6 \left(2 x - 5\right)^{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Power the outside, keep the inside, then multiply by $2$: $6 \left(2 x - 5\right)^{2}$.
U3-S1-NOTE-EX03
Section 3.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Find $\frac{dy}{dx}$ for $y=\sin{\left(4 x + 1 \right)}$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $4 \cos{\left(4 x + 1 \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate sine to cosine and multiply by the derivative of the inside: $4 \cos{\left(4 x + 1 \right)}$.
U3-S1-NOTE-YTI03
Section 3.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Find $\frac{dy}{dx}$ for $y=- \sin{\left(3 x + 1 \right)}$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $- 3 \cos{\left(3 x + 1 \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate sine to cosine and multiply by the derivative of the inside: $- 3 \cos{\left(3 x + 1 \right)}$.
U3-S1-NOTE-EX04
Section 3.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Differentiate $g(x)=e^{- 2 x - 3}$ and keep the chain factor visible.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $g'(x)=- 2 e^{- 2 x - 3}$
Solution / scoring notes: The derivative of the outer core function is evaluated at the unchanged inside, then multiplied by $-2$.
U3-S1-NOTE-YTI04
Section 3.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Differentiate $g(x)=\log{\left(- 4 x - 3 \right)}$ and keep the chain factor visible.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $g'(x)=\frac{4}{4 x + 3}$
Solution / scoring notes: The derivative of the outer core function is evaluated at the unchanged inside, then multiplied by $-4$.

Section 3.2 - Mixed Derivative Rules

U3-S2-NOTE-EX01
Section 3.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Differentiate $F(x)=\frac{\left(3 x + 1\right)^{2}}{x^{2} + 1}$. Name the outermost derivative rule you use first.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $F'(x)=\frac{2 \left(- 3 x^{2} + 8 x + 3\right)}{x^{4} + 2 x^{2} + 1}$
Solution / scoring notes: Start with the product rule, then use the chain rule inside the composite factor(s).
U3-S2-NOTE-YTI01
Section 3.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Differentiate $F(x)=\left(- x^{2} - 2 x - 1\right) \sin{\left(3 x - 1 \right)}$. Name the outermost derivative rule you use first.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $F'(x)=- 2 \left(x + 1\right) \sin{\left(3 x - 1 \right)} - 3 \left(x^{2} + 2 x + 1\right) \cos{\left(3 x - 1 \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Start with the product rule, then use the chain rule inside the composite factor(s).
U3-S2-NOTE-EX02
Section 3.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Find the derivative of $y=\left(1 - x^{2}\right) \sin{\left(3 x \right)}$ and label where the product rule and chain rule each enter.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $- 2 x \sin{\left(3 x \right)} - \left(3 x^{2} - 3\right) \cos{\left(3 x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Product rule creates two terms; differentiating $- \sin{\left(3 x \right)}$ requires the chain factor $-3$.
U3-S2-NOTE-YTI02
Section 3.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Find the derivative of $y=\left(1 - x^{2}\right) \sin{\left(2 x \right)}$ and label where the product rule and chain rule each enter.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $- 2 x \sin{\left(2 x \right)} - 2 \left(x^{2} - 1\right) \cos{\left(2 x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Product rule creates two terms; differentiating $- \sin{\left(2 x \right)}$ requires the chain factor $-2$.
U3-S2-NOTE-EX03
Section 3.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Compute $f''(x)$ for $f(x)=- 4 x^{4} - 3 x^{2} + e^{3 x}$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $f''(x)=- 48 x^{2} + 9 e^{3 x} - 6$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate once to obtain $f'(x)=- 16 x^{3} - 6 x + 3 e^{3 x}$ and differentiate again.
U3-S2-NOTE-YTI03
Section 3.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Compute $f''(x)$ for $f(x)=2 x^{4} - 3 x^{2} + e^{2 x}$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $f''(x)=24 x^{2} + 4 e^{2 x} - 6$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate once to obtain $f'(x)=8 x^{3} - 6 x + 2 e^{2 x}$ and differentiate again.
U3-S2-NOTE-EX04
Section 3.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Using the table, find $(f\circ g)'(-1)$.
$x=-1$
$g(x)$$3$
$g'(x)$$1$
$f'(3)$$2$
$f(3)$$3$

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $2$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(f\circ g)'=f'(g)g'$; the value is $2$.
U3-S2-NOTE-YTI04
Section 3.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Using the table, find $(f\circ g)'(2)$.
$x=2$
$g(x)$$-3$
$g'(x)$$-1$
$f'(-3)$$2$
$f(-3)$$-2$

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $-2$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(f\circ g)'=f'(g)g'$; the value is $-2$.

Section 3.3 - Implicit Differentiation

U3-S3-NOTE-EX01
Section 3.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

For the relation $x^2+y^2=36$, use implicit differentiation to solve for $\frac{dy}{dx}$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=-\frac{x}{y}$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate both sides: $2x+2y\frac{dy}{dx}=0$; solve for $\frac{dy}{dx}$.
U3-S3-NOTE-YTI01
Section 3.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

For the relation $x^2+y^2=9$, use implicit differentiation to solve for $\frac{dy}{dx}$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=-\frac{x}{y}$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate both sides: $2x+2y\frac{dy}{dx}=0$; solve for $\frac{dy}{dx}$.
U3-S3-NOTE-EX02
Section 3.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Differentiate implicitly and solve for $\frac{dy}{dx}$ if $xy+y^2=10$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=-\frac{y}{x+2y}$
Solution / scoring notes: Product rule on $xy$ gives $x\frac{dy}{dx}+y$; differentiating $y^2$ gives $2y\frac{dy}{dx}$. Collect the derivative terms.
U3-S3-NOTE-YTI02
Section 3.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Differentiate implicitly and solve for $\frac{dy}{dx}$ if $xy+y^2=4$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=-\frac{y}{x+2y}$
Solution / scoring notes: Product rule on $xy$ gives $x\frac{dy}{dx}+y$; differentiating $y^2$ gives $2y\frac{dy}{dx}$. Collect the derivative terms.
U3-S3-NOTE-EX03
Section 3.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

On the circle $x^2+y^2=16$, find the slope of the tangent at $(0,4)$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $0$
Solution / scoring notes: Implicit differentiation gives $dy/dx=-x/y$; substitute the point to get $0$.
U3-S3-NOTE-YTI03
Section 3.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

On the circle $x^2+y^2=16$, find the slope of the tangent at $(0,4)$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $0$
Solution / scoring notes: Implicit differentiation gives $dy/dx=-x/y$; substitute the point to get $0$.
U3-S3-NOTE-EX04
Section 3.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Find the tangent line to $x^2+y^2=25$ at $(-4,3)$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $y=\frac{4 x}{3} + \frac{25}{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: First obtain $dy/dx=-x/y$; at the point the slope is $\frac{4}{3}$, then use point-slope form.
U3-S3-NOTE-YTI04
Section 3.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Find the tangent line to $x^2+y^2=25$ at $(4,3)$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $y=\frac{25}{3} - \frac{4 x}{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: First obtain $dy/dx=-x/y$; at the point the slope is $- \frac{4}{3}$, then use point-slope form.

Section 3.4 - Inverse Derivatives

U3-S4-NOTE-EX01
Section 3.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Given $f(-1)=4$ and $f'(-1)=-4$, find $(f^{-1})'(4)$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $- \frac{1}{4}$
Solution / scoring notes: Match $f(-1)=4$, then use $(f^{-1})'(b)=1/f'(a)$.
U3-S4-NOTE-YTI01
Section 3.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Given $f(-1)=6$ and $f'(-1)=2$, find $(f^{-1})'(6)$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{1}{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Match $f(-1)=6$, then use $(f^{-1})'(b)=1/f'(a)$.
U3-S4-NOTE-EX02
Section 3.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Let $f(x)=x^3+x$ and let $g=f^{-1}$. Find $g'(2)$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{1}{4}$
Solution / scoring notes: Since $f(1)=2$, use $g'(2)=1/f'(1)=1/4$.
U3-S4-NOTE-YTI02
Section 3.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Let $f(x)=x^3+x$ and let $g=f^{-1}$. Find $g'(2)$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{1}{4}$
Solution / scoring notes: Since $f(1)=2$, use $g'(2)=1/f'(1)=1/4$.
U3-S4-NOTE-EX03
Section 3.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Differentiate $y=\operatorname{asin}{\left(3 x - 2 \right)}$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{3}{\sqrt{1 - \left(3 x - 2\right)^{2}}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $d(\arcsin u)/dx=u'/\sqrt{1-u^2}$ with $u=3 x - 2$.
U3-S4-NOTE-YTI03
Section 3.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Differentiate $y=\operatorname{asin}{\left(3 x - 2 \right)}$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{3}{\sqrt{1 - \left(3 x - 2\right)^{2}}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $d(\arcsin u)/dx=u'/\sqrt{1-u^2}$ with $u=3 x - 2$.
U3-S4-NOTE-EX04
Section 3.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Find $\frac{d}{dx}$ of $\operatorname{atan}{\left(3 x^{2} - 2 \right)}$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $\frac{6 x}{\left(3 x^{2} - 2\right)^{2} + 1}$
Solution / scoring notes: For $\arctan u$, use $u'/(1+u^2)$ and include $6 x$.
U3-S4-NOTE-YTI04
Section 3.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Find $\frac{d}{dx}$ of $\operatorname{atan}{\left(x^{2} - 1 \right)}$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $\frac{2 x}{\left(x^{2} - 1\right)^{2} + 1}$
Solution / scoring notes: For $\arctan u$, use $u'/(1+u^2)$ and include $2 x$.

Section 3.5 - Derivative Strategy and Review

U3-S5-NOTE-EX01
Section 3.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Compute $f''(x)$ for $f(x)=2 x^{4} + \sin{\left(3 x \right)}$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $f''(x)=24 x^{2} - 9 \sin{\left(3 x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Compute the first derivative with the needed chain factor, then differentiate again.
U3-S5-NOTE-YTI01
Section 3.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Compute $f''(x)$ for $f(x)=2 x^{4} + \sin{\left(3 x \right)}$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $f''(x)=24 x^{2} - 9 \sin{\left(3 x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Compute the first derivative with the needed chain factor, then differentiate again.
U3-S5-NOTE-EX02
Section 3.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 3

Example

Create a rule sequence for differentiating $H(x)=\frac{\left(3 x - 1\right)^{4}}{x^{2} + 1}$, then compute $H'(x)$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $H'(x)=\frac{2 \left(3 x - 1\right)^{3} \left(6 x^{2} - x \left(3 x - 1\right) + 6\right)}{\left(x^{2} + 1\right)^{2}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Identify the outermost structure first, then work inward. A correct sequence produces $\frac{2 \left(3 x - 1\right)^{3} \left(6 x^{2} - x \left(3 x - 1\right) + 6\right)}{\left(x^{2} + 1\right)^{2}}$.
U3-S5-NOTE-YTI02
Section 3.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 3

You Try It

Create a rule sequence for differentiating $H(x)=\left(x^{2} + x - 2\right) \sin{\left(3 x - 3 \right)}$, then compute $H'(x)$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $H'(x)=\left(2 x + 1\right) \sin{\left(3 x - 3 \right)} + 3 \left(x^{2} + x - 2\right) \cos{\left(3 x - 3 \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Identify the outermost structure first, then work inward. A correct sequence produces $\left(2 x + 1\right) \sin{\left(3 x - 3 \right)} + 3 \left(x^{2} + x - 2\right) \cos{\left(3 x - 3 \right)}$.
U3-S5-NOTE-EX03
Section 3.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A particle has position $s(t)=t^{3} - 3 t^{2} + t$. Find its velocity and acceleration at $t=1$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $v(1)=-2$, $a(1)=0$
Solution / scoring notes: Velocity is the first derivative and acceleration is the second derivative: $v(t)=3 t^{2} - 6 t + 1$, $a(t)=6 \left(t - 1\right)$.
U3-S5-NOTE-YTI03
Section 3.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A particle has position $s(t)=t^{3} - 3 t^{2} + t$. Find its velocity and acceleration at $t=2$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $v(2)=1$, $a(2)=6$
Solution / scoring notes: Velocity is the first derivative and acceleration is the second derivative: $v(t)=3 t^{2} - 6 t + 1$, $a(t)=6 \left(t - 1\right)$.
U3-S5-NOTE-EX04
Section 3.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

For $F(x)=\left(x^{2} + 1\right) e^{2 x}$, find $F'(0)$ and $F''(0)$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $2$, $6$
Solution / scoring notes: Use product and chain rules for the first derivative, then differentiate again and evaluate.
U3-S5-NOTE-YTI04
Section 3.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

For $F(x)=\left(x^{2} + 1\right) e^{2 x}$, find $F'(1)$ and $F''(1)$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $6 e^{2}$, $18 e^{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use product and chain rules for the first derivative, then differentiate again and evaluate.

U3-S1-PS1-L02

Differentiate $f(x)=- 4 x - 2 \log{\left(x \right)}$ using the appropriate core derivative rules.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $f'(x)=-4 - \frac{2}{x}$

U3-S1-PS1-L03
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
Find the derivative of $F(x)=\left(2 x^{2} - 3\right) \sin{\left(x \right)}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $F'(x)=4 x \sin{\left(x \right)} + \left(2 x^{2} - 3\right) \cos{\left(x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule $(uv)'=u'v+uv'$ and simplify.
U3-S1-PS1-L04
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
Differentiate $Q(x)=\frac{2 x + e^{x}}{x^{2} + 2}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $Q'(x)=\frac{- 2 x \left(2 x + e^{x}\right) + \left(x^{2} + 2\right) \left(e^{x} + 2\right)}{\left(x^{2} + 2\right)^{2}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the quotient rule and simplify to $\frac{- 2 x \left(2 x + e^{x}\right) + \left(x^{2} + 2\right) \left(e^{x} + 2\right)}{\left(x^{2} + 2\right)^{2}}$.
U3-S1-PS1-L05
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
Use the table to compute $(fg)'(1)$.
$x=1$
$f(x)$$1$
$f'(x)$$-3$
$g(x)$$-4$
$g'(x)$$1$

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $13$
Solution / scoring notes: Product rule gives $-3(-4)+(1)(1)=13$.
U3-S1-PS1-M01
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Find the average rate of change of $g(x)=3 x^{2} - 2 x - 3$ on $[-5,-2]$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-23$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{g(b)-g(a)}{b-a}$. Substitution gives $-23$.
U3-S1-PS1-M02
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
Find the slope of the line through $(-7,-4)$ and $(1,-2)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{1}{4}$
Solution / scoring notes: Compute rise over run: $\frac{-2--4}{1--7}=\frac{1}{4}$.
U3-S1-PS1-M03
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
The function $h(x)=\frac{x^{3} + 27}{x + 3}$ is defined for $x\ne -3$. What value should be assigned to $h(-3)$ so the extended function is continuous?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $27$
Solution / scoring notes: Cancel the factor $x + 3$ for $x\ne -3$. The simplified expression has limit $27$ at $x=-3$.
U3-S1-PS1-M04
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
Given $p(6)=4$ and $q(6)=-9$, find $(p-q)(6)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $13$
Solution / scoring notes: Subtract the function values: $4-(-9)=13$.
U3-S1-PS1-M05
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
For $f(x)=5 x + 1$, evaluate $f(-5)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-24$
Solution / scoring notes: Substitute $x=-5$: $f(-5)=-24$.
U3-S1-PS1-M06
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
Solve $7x-10=5$ for $x$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $x=\frac{15}{7}$
Solution / scoring notes: Isolate $x$: $x = \frac{15}{7}$, so $x=\frac{15}{7}$.
U3-S1-PS1-M07
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Evaluate $\lim_{x\to -7}\frac{x^{4} - 2401}{x + 7}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-1372$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor the difference of powers and cancel the factor $x + 7$. The remaining expression approaches $-1372$.
U3-S1-PS1-M08
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
Over the interval $[-5,-3]$, determine $\frac{\Delta g}{\Delta x}$ for $g(x)=4 x^{2} + 4 x - 5$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-28$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $\frac{g(b)-g(a)}{b-a}$. Substitution gives $-28$.
U3-S1-PS1-M09
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 1
Find the slope of the line through $(-7,-7)$ and $(8,8)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $1$
Solution / scoring notes: Compute rise over run: $\frac{8--7}{8--7}=1$.
U3-S1-PS1-M10

The function $h(x)=\frac{x^{4} - 4096}{x + 8}$ is defined for $x\ne -8$. What value should be assigned to $h(-8)$ so the extended function is continuous?

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $-2048$
Solution / scoring notes: Cancel the factor $x + 8$ for $x\ne -8$. The simplified expression has limit $-2048$ at $x=-8$.

Section 3.2 - Mixed Derivative Rules

U3-S2-PS1-C01
Section 3.2 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Differentiate $F(x)=\left(x^{2} + 2 x - 2\right) e^{2 x + 1}$. Name the outermost derivative rule you use first.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $F'(x)=2 \left(x^{2} + 3 x - 1\right) e^{2 x + 1}$
Solution / scoring notes: Start with the product rule, then use the chain rule inside the composite factor(s).

Solution / scoring notes: Compute the first derivative with the needed chain factor, then differentiate again.
U3-S5-PS1-C02

Create a rule sequence for differentiating $H(x)=\left(x^{2} - 2 x + 3\right) e^{2 x - 1}$, then compute $H'(x)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $H'(x)=2 \left(x^{2} - x + 2\right) e^{2 x - 1}$
Solution / scoring notes: Identify the outermost structure first, then work inward. A correct sequence produces $2 \left(x^{2} - x + 2\right) e^{2 x - 1}$.
U3-S5-PS1-C03
Section 3.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
A particle has position $s(t)=t^{3} - 2 t^{2} + t$. Find its velocity and acceleration at $t=1$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $v(1)=0$, $a(1)=2$
Solution / scoring notes: Velocity is the first derivative and acceleration is the second derivative: $v(t)=3 t^{2} - 4 t + 1$, $a(t)=2 \left(3 t - 2\right)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $6 e^{2}$, $18 e^{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use product and chain rules for the first derivative, then differentiate again and evaluate.
U3-S5-PS1-C05

A student differentiates $(x^2+1)^4e^x$ by applying only the product rule and never differentiating the inside $x^2+1$. Diagnose the structural error and describe the correct rule sequence.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Use product rule on the two factors, and chain rule inside the derivative of $(x^2+1)^4$.
Solution / scoring notes: The outer product structure does not eliminate the inner composition; both rules are required.
U3-S5-PS1-L01

Explain the difference between $f^{-1}(x)$ and $1/f(x)$. Then state which expression appears in the inverse-function derivative relationship.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $f^{-1}$ means inverse function, not reciprocal; $(f^{-1})'(a)=1/f'(f^{-1}(a))$.
Solution / scoring notes: The superscript $-1$ on a function denotes composition inverse when it is attached to the function name.

Differentiate $y=\operatorname{asin}{\left(2 x + 2 \right)}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{2}{\sqrt{1 - 4 \left(x + 1\right)^{2}}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $d(\arcsin u)/dx=u'/\sqrt{1-u^2}$ with $u=2 x + 2$.
U3-S5-PS1-L05
Section 3.5 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 2
Find $\frac{d}{dx}$ of $\operatorname{atan}{\left(2 x^{2} - 2 \right)}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{4 x}{4 \left(x^{2} - 1\right)^{2} + 1}$
Solution / scoring notes: For $\arctan u$, use $u'/(1+u^2)$ and include $4 x$.

Practice Set 2

Section 3.1 - Chain Rule

U3-S1-PS2-C01
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Find $\frac{dy}{dx}$ for $y=\sin{\left(3 x + 1 \right)}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $3 \cos{\left(3 x + 1 \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate sine to cosine and multiply by the derivative of the inside: $3 \cos{\left(3 x + 1 \right)}$.
U3-S1-PS2-C02
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Differentiate $g(x)=e^{3 x - 3}$ and keep the chain factor visible.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $g'(x)=3 e^{3 x - 3}$
Solution / scoring notes: The derivative of the outer core function is evaluated at the unchanged inside, then multiplied by $3$.
U3-S1-PS2-C03
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Differentiate the nested function $h(x)=\left(3 x^{2} - 1\right)^{4}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $h'(x)=24 x \left(3 x^{2} - 1\right)^{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the chain rule to the outer power and multiply by $6 x$.
U3-S1-PS2-C04
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Use the table to compute $(f\circ g)'(2)$.
QuantityValue
$g(2)$$3$
$f'(3)$$-2$
$g'(2)$$3$

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $-6$
Solution / scoring notes: Chain rule: $f'(g(2))g'(2)=(-2)(3)=-6$.
U3-S1-PS2-C05
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
For $y=\left(2 x - 1\right)^{2}$, identify an inner function and an outer function, then state the factor contributed by differentiating the inner function.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Inner: $1 - 2 x$; outer: $u^2$; inner-derivative factor: $-2$.
Solution / scoring notes: Trace $x\to 1 - 2 x\to (1 - 2 x)^2$; the chain rule multiplies by $-2$.
U3-S1-PS2-L01

Find the derivative of $F(x)=\left(4 x - 2\right) e^{x}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $F'(x)=\left(4 x + 2\right) e^{x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule $(uv)'=u'v+uv'$ and simplify.
U3-S1-PS2-L02
Section 3.1 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 2
Differentiate $Q(x)=\frac{4 \left(x - 1\right)}{x^{2} - 2}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $Q'(x)=\frac{4 \left(x^{2} - 2 x \left(x - 1\right) - 2\right)}{\left(x^{2} - 2\right)^{2}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the quotient rule and simplify to $\frac{4 \left(x^{2} - 2 x \left(x - 1\right) - 2\right)}{\left(x^{2} - 2\right)^{2}}$.

Section 3.2 - Mixed Derivative Rules

U3-S2-PS2-C01
Section 3.2 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Compute $f''(x)$ for $f(x)=- 4 x^{4} + 2 x^{2} + e^{2 x}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $f''(x)=- 48 x^{2} + 4 e^{2 x} + 4$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate once to obtain $f'(x)=- 16 x^{3} + 4 x + 2 e^{2 x}$ and differentiate again.
U3-S2-PS2-C02

$x=1$
$g(x)$$-3$
$g'(x)$$2$
$f'(-3)$$-1$
$f(-3)$$-4$

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $-2$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(f\circ g)'=f'(g)g'$; the value is $-2$.
U3-S2-PS2-C03
Section 3.2 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
Before differentiating $R(x)=x^{2} + 3$, choose an efficient valid strategy and compute $R'(x)$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $R'(x)=2 x$
Solution / scoring notes: Simplify to $x^{2} + 3$ before applying basic rules. Quotient rule is valid but inefficient.
U3-S2-PS2-C04
Section 3.2 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
For $P(x)=\left(x^{2} + 1\right) e^{2 x}$, compute $P'(x)$ and then $P''(x)$. Keep the rule structure organized.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $P'(x)=2 \left(x^{2} + x + 1\right) e^{2 x}$, $P''(x)=\left(4 x^{2} + 8 x + 6\right) e^{2 x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use product plus chain rule for the first derivative, then differentiate each first-derivative term for the second.
U3-S2-PS2-C05
Section 3.2 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Differentiate $F(x)=\left(- x^{2} + 2 x - 3\right) \sin{\left(3 x - 1 \right)}$. Name the outermost derivative rule you use first.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $F'(x)=\left(2 - 2 x\right) \sin{\left(3 x - 1 \right)} + \left(- 3 x^{2} + 6 x - 9\right) \cos{\left(3 x - 1 \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Start with the product rule, then use the chain rule inside the composite factor(s).

Section 3.3 - Implicit Differentiation

U3-S3-PS2-C01
Section 3.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
On the circle $x^2+y^2=9$, find the slope of the tangent at $(0,3)$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $0$
Solution / scoring notes: Implicit differentiation gives $dy/dx=-x/y$; substitute the point to get $0$.
U3-S3-PS2-C02
Section 3.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Find the tangent line to $x^2+y^2=25$ at $(0,5)$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $y=5$
Solution / scoring notes: First obtain $dy/dx=-x/y$; at the point the slope is $0$, then use point-slope form.
U3-S3-PS2-C03
Section 3.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
Differentiate each term with respect to $x$: $x^3$, $y^3$, and $xy$. Identify every place a $\frac{dy}{dx}$ factor appears.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $3x^2$, $3y^2\frac{dy}{dx}$, and $y+x\frac{dy}{dx}$.
Solution / scoring notes: Terms containing $y$ require the chain rule because $y$ depends on $x$.
U3-S3-PS2-C04
Section 3.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
For the relation $x^2+y^2=25$, compare solving explicitly for the upper branch with using implicit differentiation to find the tangent slope at $(3,4)$. Which method is more direct, and why?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Implicit differentiation is more direct; it avoids solving for a branch and gives $dy/dx=-x/y$, so the slope is $-3/4$.
Solution / scoring notes: A strong comparison recognizes both methods are valid on the upper branch but implicit differentiation preserves the relation and is shorter.
U3-S3-PS2-C05
Section 3.3 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
For the relation $x^2+y^2=25$, use implicit differentiation to solve for $\frac{dy}{dx}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=-\frac{x}{y}$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate both sides: $2x+2y\frac{dy}{dx}=0$; solve for $\frac{dy}{dx}$.
U3-S3-PS2-L01
Section 3.3 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 2
Find the derivative of $y=\left(2 - x^{2}\right) \sin{\left(2 x \right)}$ and label where the product rule and chain rule each enter.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $- 2 x \sin{\left(2 x \right)} - 2 \left(x^{2} - 2\right) \cos{\left(2 x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Product rule creates two terms; differentiating $- \sin{\left(2 x \right)}$ requires the chain factor $-2$.

Section 3.4 - Inverse Derivatives

U3-S4-PS2-C01
Section 3.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Differentiate $y=\operatorname{asin}{\left(2 x + 1 \right)}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\frac{1}{\sqrt{x \left(- x - 1\right)}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $d(\arcsin u)/dx=u'/\sqrt{1-u^2}$ with $u=2 x + 1$.
U3-S4-PS2-C02
Section 3.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Find $\frac{d}{dx}$ of $\operatorname{atan}{\left(2 x^{2} - 2 \right)}$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Solution / scoring notes: For $\arctan u$, use $u'/(1+u^2)$ and include $4 x$.
U3-S4-PS2-C03
Section 3.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
The graph shows a one-to-one function $f$ and its inverse $g$. Explain how the tangent slopes at corresponding reflected points are related when neither slope is zero.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: The slopes are reciprocals: $g'(f(a))=1/f'(a)$.
Solution / scoring notes: Inverse graphs reflect across $y=x$; the derivative relationship follows from differentiating $f(g(x))=x$.
U3-S4-PS2-C04

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $f^{-1}$ means inverse function, not reciprocal; $(f^{-1})'(a)=1/f'(f^{-1}(a))$.
Solution / scoring notes: The superscript $-1$ on a function denotes composition inverse when it is attached to the function name.

Differentiate implicitly and solve for $\frac{dy}{dx}$ if $xy+y^2=8$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Solution / scoring notes: Product rule on $xy$ gives $x\frac{dy}{dx}+y$; differentiating $y^2$ gives $2y\frac{dy}{dx}$. Collect the derivative terms.

Solution / scoring notes: Implicit differentiation gives $dy/dx=-x/y$; substitute the point to get $- \frac{4}{3}$.

Section 3.5 - Derivative Strategy and Review

U3-S5-PS2-C01

A particle has position $s(t)=t^{3} - 2 t^{2} + t$. Find its velocity and acceleration at $t=2$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $v(2)=5$, $a(2)=8$
Solution / scoring notes: Velocity is the first derivative and acceleration is the second derivative: $v(t)=3 t^{2} - 4 t + 1$, $a(t)=2 \left(3 t - 2\right)$.

Answer: $2$, $6$
Solution / scoring notes: Use product and chain rules for the first derivative, then differentiate again and evaluate.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Use product rule on the two factors, and chain rule inside the derivative of $(x^2+1)^4$.
Solution / scoring notes: The outer product structure does not eliminate the inner composition; both rules are required.

Extra Practice

Section 3.1 - Chain Rule

U3-S1-XP-C01
Section 3.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
For $y=- \left(4 x - 3\right)^{3}$, identify an inner function and an outer function, then state the factor contributed by differentiating the inner function.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Inner: $3 - 4 x$; outer: $u^3$; inner-derivative factor: $-4$.
Solution / scoring notes: Trace $x\to 3 - 4 x\to (3 - 4 x)^3$; the chain rule multiplies by $-4$.
U3-S1-XP-C02
Section 3.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
Differentiate $f(x)=\left(4 x + 1\right)^{2}$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $f'(x)=32 x + 8$

Solution / scoring notes: Use $d(\arcsin u)/dx=u'/\sqrt{1-u^2}$ with $u=- 2 x - 1$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: The slopes are reciprocals: $g'(f(a))=1/f'(a)$.
Solution / scoring notes: Inverse graphs reflect across $y=x$; the derivative relationship follows from differentiating $f(g(x))=x$.

Solution / scoring notes: A strong comparison recognizes both methods are valid on the upper branch but implicit differentiation preserves the relation and is shorter.

Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule $(uv)'=u'v+uv'$ and simplify.

Solution / scoring notes: Use the quotient rule and simplify to $\frac{2 \left(x^{2} - 2 x \left(x + 1\right) - 4\right)}{\left(x^{2} - 4\right)^{2}}$.

Section 3.3 - Implicit Differentiation

U3-S3-WU1-C01
Section 3.3 | Warm Up 1 | Current | DOK 3
For the relation $x^2+y^2=25$, compare solving explicitly for the upper branch with using implicit differentiation to find the tangent slope at $(3,4)$. Which method is more direct, and why?

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Answer: Implicit differentiation is more direct; it avoids solving for a branch and gives $dy/dx=-x/y$, so the slope is $-3/4$.
Solution / scoring notes: A strong comparison recognizes both methods are valid on the upper branch but implicit differentiation preserves the relation and is shorter.

$x=1$

$g'(x)$$4$
$f'(2)$$-3$
$f(2)$$3$

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Answer: $-12$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $(f\circ g)'=f'(g)g'$; the value is $-12$.

Section 3.4 - Inverse Derivatives

U3-S4-WU1-C01
Section 3.4 | Warm Up 1 | Current | DOK 1
Explain the difference between $f^{-1}(x)$ and $1/f(x)$. Then state which expression appears in the inverse-function derivative relationship.

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Answer: $f^{-1}$ means inverse function, not reciprocal; $(f^{-1})'(a)=1/f'(f^{-1}(a))$.
Solution / scoring notes: The superscript $-1$ on a function denotes composition inverse when it is attached to the function name.
U3-S4-WU1-L01
Section 3.4 | Warm Up 1 | Last | DOK 2

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Answer: $y=\frac{25}{4} - \frac{3 x}{4}$
Solution / scoring notes: First obtain $dy/dx=-x/y$; at the point the slope is $- \frac{3}{4}$, then use point-slope form.

Differentiate $F(x)=\left(- x^{2} + x - 3\right) \sin{\left(2 x + 3 \right)}$. Name the outermost derivative rule you use first.

Answer: $F'(x)=\left(1 - 2 x\right) \sin{\left(2 x + 3 \right)} + 2 \left(- x^{2} + x - 3\right) \cos{\left(2 x + 3 \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Start with the product rule, then use the chain rule inside the composite factor(s).

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: $-12$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor the difference of powers and cancel the factor $x + 6$. The remaining expression approaches $-12$.
U3-S3-ET-B-C01

For the relation $x^2+y^2=25$, compare solving explicitly for the upper branch with using implicit differentiation to find the tangent slope at $(3,4)$. Which method is more direct, and why?

Use precise notation in the final response.

Answer: Implicit differentiation is more direct; it avoids solving for a branch and gives $dy/dx=-x/y$, so the slope is $-3/4$.
Solution / scoring notes: A strong comparison recognizes both methods are valid on the upper branch but implicit differentiation preserves the relation and is shorter.

Use precise notation in the final response.

Answer: $\frac{dy}{dx}=-\frac{x}{y}$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate both sides: $2x+2y\frac{dy}{dx}=0$; solve for $\frac{dy}{dx}$.

Section 3.4 - Inverse Derivatives

U3-S4-ET-A-C01
Section 3.4 | Exit Ticket A | Current | DOK 2
The graph shows a one-to-one function $f$ and its inverse $g$. Explain how the tangent slopes at corresponding reflected points are related when neither slope is zero.

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: The slopes are reciprocals: $g'(f(a))=1/f'(a)$.
Solution / scoring notes: Inverse graphs reflect across $y=x$; the derivative relationship follows from differentiating $f(g(x))=x$.
U3-S4-ET-A-C02
Section 3.4 | Exit Ticket A | Current | DOK 1
Explain the difference between $f^{-1}(x)$ and $1/f(x)$. Then state which expression appears in the inverse-function derivative relationship.

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: $f^{-1}$ means inverse function, not reciprocal; $(f^{-1})'(a)=1/f'(f^{-1}(a))$.
Solution / scoring notes: The superscript $-1$ on a function denotes composition inverse when it is attached to the function name.

Solution / scoring notes: The outer product structure does not eliminate the inner composition; both rules are required.

9. Performance / Future Tasks

Section 3.1 - Chain Rule

U3-S1-PERF01
Section 3.1 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 3

Performance / Future Task

Create a chain-rule tracing method that identifies each outer/inner layer before differentiating. Apply it to $(3x-1)^4$, $\sin(x^2+2)$, $e^{5x}$, $\ln(2x+3)$, compare where the method succeeds or needs refinement, and state one rule that prevents a missing inner derivative.
Expected evidence: Correct structure traces; correct derivatives; transferable rule; notation clarity.
Scoring criteria: Correct structure traces; correct derivatives; transferable rule; notation clarity.
Estimated student time: 10-12 minutes
Answer: A valid method works from the outside inward and multiplies by the derivative of each successive inner layer.
Solution / scoring notes: A strong response traces all four structures, differentiates them correctly, and articulates a reusable outside-to-inside rule.

Section 3.2 - Mixed Derivative Rules

U3-S2-PERF01
Section 3.2 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 3

Performance / Future Task

Compare two valid pathways for differentiating $R(x)=\frac{x(x^2+1)^3}{x^2}$. One pathway begins with quotient/product rules; the other simplifies first. Carry out both far enough to verify agreement, then defend the more efficient pathway using structural evidence.
Expected evidence: Two valid setups; correct derivative; comparison of algebraic load; defended efficiency claim.
Scoring criteria: Two valid setups; correct derivative; comparison of algebraic load; defended efficiency claim.
Estimated student time: 12-15 minutes
Answer: $R'(x)=\frac{\left(x^{2} + 1\right)^{2} \left(5 x^{2} - 1\right)}{x^{2}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Simplifying first gives $R(x)=\frac{\left(x^{2} + 1\right)^{3}}{x}$; differentiating that form avoids a nested quotient/product expansion. Both valid routes should simplify to the same derivative.

Section 3.3 - Implicit Differentiation

U3-S3-PERF01
Section 3.3 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Performance / Future Task

Investigate tangent behavior on $x^2+y^2=25$. Derive $dy/dx$, evaluate it at the four listed points, describe sign and magnitude patterns, and revise an initial conjecture about how tangent slope changes around the upper semicircle.
PointDerivative value
$(3,4)$$- \frac{3}{4}$
$(4,3)$$- \frac{4}{3}$
$(-3,4)$$\frac{3}{4}$
$(-4,3)$$\frac{4}{3}$
Expected evidence: General derivative; four evaluations; pattern statement; revised conjecture supported by evidence.
Scoring criteria: General derivative; four evaluations; pattern statement; revised conjecture supported by evidence.
Estimated student time: 15-18 minutes
Answer: $dy/dx=-x/y$
Solution / scoring notes: The derivative is negative on the upper-right arc, positive on the upper-left arc, and its magnitude grows as the point approaches the side of the circle where $|y|$ becomes smaller.

Section 3.4 - Inverse Derivatives

U3-S4-PERF01
Section 3.4 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Performance / Future Task

Use the paired graph of a one-to-one function $f$ and its inverse $g$ to build and test a derivative relationship. Explain the reflection of corresponding points, derive the reciprocal-slope rule from $f(g(x))=x$, and identify a condition under which the reciprocal formula cannot be used directly.
Expected evidence: Graph correspondence; chain-rule derivation; reciprocal relationship; nonzero-slope condition.
Scoring criteria: Graph correspondence; chain-rule derivation; reciprocal relationship; nonzero-slope condition.
Estimated student time: 15-18 minutes
Answer: $g'(x)=1/f'(g(x))$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate $f(g(x))=x$ to obtain $f'(g(x))g'(x)=1$. The reciprocal rule requires the corresponding derivative in the denominator to be nonzero.

Section 3.5 - Derivative Strategy and Review

U3-S5-PERF01
Section 3.5 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Performance / Future Task

Build, test, and refine a derivative-strategy decision guide using these four cases: $(2x-1)^5$, $(x^2+1)e^{3x}$, $x^2+xy+y^2=9$, $(f^{-1})'(4)$. For each case, name the first structural question your guide asks, carry out the needed derivative reasoning, record any failure of the guide, and revise it into a final procedure that distinguishes composition, product/quotient, implicit, and inverse relationships.
Expected evidence: Initial guide; four tests; documented revision; final transferable guide; mathematically correct reasoning.
Scoring criteria: Initial guide; four tests; documented revision; final transferable guide; mathematically correct reasoning.
Estimated student time: 18-22 minutes
Answer: A successful guide begins with structure: explicit vs implicit/inverse, then outer product/quotient vs composition, and works inward while preserving notation.
Solution / scoring notes: The task is scored on the tested decision process, correct sample reasoning, documented revision, and a final guide that handles all four families.

Answer: $4y^3\frac{dy}{dx}$, $y+x\frac{dy}{dx}$, $\cos(y)\frac{dy}{dx}$.
Solution / scoring notes: Each occurrence of $y$ is a dependent quantity.

DOK 1 - Inverse Derivative Recognition

U3-FA-D1-04-V1
Section 3.4 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 1

Inverse Derivative Recognition

Which relationship is valid when the required derivative is nonzero?
  1. $(f^{-1})'(a)=\frac{1}{f'(f^{-1}(a))}$
  2. $(f^{-1})'(a)=f'(a)$
  3. $(f^{-1})'(a)=\frac{1}{f'(a)}$
  4. $(f^{-1})'(a)=f^{-1}(f'(a))$
Answer: A. $(f^{-1})'(a)=\frac{1}{f'(f^{-1}(a))}$
Solution / scoring notes: The inverse derivative uses the derivative at the corresponding inverse input.

DOK 2 - Mixed Derivative Procedure

U3-FA-D2-02-V1
Section 3.2 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Mixed Derivative Procedure

Differentiate $F(x)=\left(x^{2} - x - 2\right) e^{2 - 2 x}$. State the outermost rule first.
Answer: $F'(x)=\left(- 2 x^{2} + 4 x + 3\right) e^{2 - 2 x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the outer structure rule and chain rule inside composite factors.
U3-FA-D2-02-V2
Section 3.2 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Mixed Derivative Procedure

Differentiate $F(x)=\left(x^{2} - 2 x + 1\right) \sin{\left(2 x + 1 \right)}$. State the outermost rule first.
Answer: $F'(x)=2 \left(x - 1\right) \sin{\left(2 x + 1 \right)} + 2 \left(x^{2} - 2 x + 1\right) \cos{\left(2 x + 1 \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the outer structure rule and chain rule inside composite factors.
U3-FA-D2-02-V3
Section 3.2 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Mixed Derivative Procedure

Differentiate $F(x)=\frac{\left(3 x - 1\right)^{4}}{x^{2} + 2}$. State the outermost rule first.
Answer: $F'(x)=\frac{2 \left(3 x - 1\right)^{3} \left(6 x^{2} - x \left(3 x - 1\right) + 12\right)}{\left(x^{2} + 2\right)^{2}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the outer structure rule and chain rule inside composite factors.

Mixed Derivative Procedure

Differentiate $F(x)=\frac{\left(2 x - 3\right)^{2}}{x^{2} + 3}$. State the outermost rule first.
Answer: $F'(x)=\frac{6 \left(2 x^{2} + x - 6\right)}{x^{4} + 6 x^{2} + 9}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the outer structure rule and chain rule inside composite factors.
U3-FA-D2-02-V5
Section 3.2 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Mixed Derivative Procedure

Answer: $F'(x)=- 2 \left(x + 1\right) \sin{\left(3 x + 1 \right)} - 3 \left(x^{2} + 2 x + 3\right) \cos{\left(3 x + 1 \right)}$

Derivative Strategy Defense

Two students propose different first steps for $H(x)=\left(x^{2} + 3\right)^{3}$: simplify first, or use product/quotient rules immediately. Choose and defend an efficient pathway, then compute $H'(x)$.
Answer: $H'(x)=6 x \left(x^{2} + 3\right)^{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Simplifying to $\left(x^{2} + 3\right)^{3}$ reduces structural complexity before differentiation.

DOK 3 - Implicit Solution Critique

U3-FA-D3-02-V1
Section 3.3 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

Implicit Solution Critique

A student differentiates $x^2+xy+y^2=12$ as $2x+x+2y=0$. Identify every structural error, repair the derivative equation, and solve for $dy/dx$.
Answer: $dy/dx=-(2x+y)/(x+2y)$
Solution / scoring notes: The product $xy$ requires product rule, and every derivative of a $y$ term needs a $dy/dx$ factor.
U3-FA-D3-02-V2
Section 3.3 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 3

Implicit Solution Critique

A student differentiates $x^2+xy+y^2=12$ as $2x+x+2y=0$. Identify every structural error, repair the derivative equation, and solve for $dy/dx$.

Make the mathematical conclusion visible without relying on a cross-reference. Focus the response on the implicit critique requested here.

Answer: $dy/dx=-(2x+y)/(x+2y)$
Solution / scoring notes: The product $xy$ requires product rule, and every derivative of a $y$ term needs a $dy/dx$ factor.

Higher-Order Strategy Task

Plan an efficient route to $f''(x)$ for $f(x)=\left(x^{2} + 1\right) e^{2 x}$. Explain how your notation prevents losing terms, then compute the second derivative.
Answer: $f''(x)=\left(4 x^{2} + 8 x + 6\right) e^{2 x}$

DOK 4 - Inverse Relationship Model

U3-FA-D4-03-V1
Section 3.4 | Formative Assessment | Performance | DOK 4

Inverse Relationship Model

Construct a multi-representation explanation of why corresponding derivative slopes for a function and inverse are reciprocal. Use the graph, the identity $f(f^{-1}(x))=x$, and one numerical example; then state when the reciprocal relationship fails to produce a finite inverse derivative.
Expected evidence: Performance evidence aligned to assessment-plan DOK 4 family.
Scoring criteria: Score for completion of all requested evidence, mathematical accuracy, and justified conclusions.
Estimated student time: 12-18 minutes
Answer: $(f^{-1})'(x)=1/f'(f^{-1}(x))$
Solution / scoring notes: Use graphical reflection, chain-rule derivation, a numerical check, and the nonzero-denominator condition.

Solution / scoring notes: Use graphical reflection, chain-rule derivation, a numerical check, and the nonzero-denominator condition.

Derivative Synthesis Performance

Analyze the family $F_a(x)=(x^2+a)^3e^{2x}$ for $a=1,2,3$. Develop a reusable differentiation strategy, compare how the parameter changes values but not rule structure, verify one derivative numerically at a chosen point, and communicate a final strategy that transfers to a new value of $a$.
Expected evidence: Performance evidence aligned to assessment-plan DOK 4 family.
Scoring criteria: Score for completion of all requested evidence, mathematical accuracy, and justified conclusions.
Estimated student time: 12-18 minutes
Answer: The reusable strategy is product rule outside and chain rule within $(x^2+a)^3$; parameter changes do not alter the rule sequence.
Solution / scoring notes: Score symbolic family derivative, comparison across parameter values, numerical verification, and transfer statement.

11. Summative Assessment Source Content

Summative Version 1

U3-SUM-V1-Q01
Section 3.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Find the derivative of $f(x)=\left(2 x + 1\right)^{2}$.
  1. $8 x + 2$
  2. $8 x + 4$
  3. $- 8 x - 4$
  4. $- 4 x - 2$
Answer: B. $8 x + 4$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the chain rule, including the derivative of the inside.
U3-SUM-V1-Q02
Section 3.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Differentiate $y=- \sin{\left(4 x - 3 \right)}$.
Answer: $- 4 \cos{\left(4 x - 3 \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Chain rule gives $- 4 \cos{\left(4 x - 3 \right)}$.
U3-SUM-V1-Q03
Section 3.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Use the table to find $(f\circ g)'(2)$.
QuantityValue
$g(2)$$2$
$f'(2)$$-4$
$g'(2)$$3$
  1. $3$
  2. $-1$
  3. $-4$
  4. $-12$
Answer: D. $-12$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $f'(g(a))g'(a)$.
U3-SUM-V1-Q04
Section 3.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Explain why differentiating $(x^2+1)^5$ as $5(x^2+1)^4$ is incomplete, and repair the derivative.
Answer: $10x(x^2+1)^4$
Solution / scoring notes: The missing factor is the derivative of the inside, $2x$.
U3-SUM-V1-Q05
Section 3.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Which expression equals the derivative of $F(x)=\frac{\left(2 x + 1\right)^{2}}{x^{2} + 3}$?
  1. $\frac{x^{4} + 2 x^{2} + 22 x + 21}{x^{4} + 6 x^{2} + 9}$
  2. $\frac{2 \left(- 2 x^{2} + 11 x + 6\right)}{x^{4} + 6 x^{2} + 9}$
  3. $\frac{2 \left(2 x^{2} - 11 x - 6\right)}{x^{4} + 6 x^{2} + 9}$
  4. $\frac{\left(2 x + 1\right)^{2}}{x^{2} + 3}$
Answer: B. $\frac{2 \left(- 2 x^{2} + 11 x + 6\right)}{x^{4} + 6 x^{2} + 9}$
Solution / scoring notes: Coordinate the outer product/quotient rule with inner chain factors.
U3-SUM-V1-Q06
Section 3.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Differentiate $P(x)=\left(x^{2} + 1\right) e^{2 x}$.
Answer: $P'(x)=2 \left(x^{2} + x + 1\right) e^{2 x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use product rule and chain rule on the exponential.
U3-SUM-V1-Q07
Section 3.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Find $f''(x)$ for $f(x)=3 x^{4} - 2 x^{2} + e^{2 x}$.
  1. $36 x^{2} + x + 4 e^{2 x} - 4$
  2. $12 x^{3} - 4 x + 2 e^{2 x}$
  3. $36 x^{2} + 4 e^{2 x} - 4$
  4. $- 36 x^{2} - 4 e^{2 x} + 4$
Answer: C. $36 x^{2} + 4 e^{2 x} - 4$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate twice and preserve higher-order notation.
U3-SUM-V1-Q08
Section 3.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Choose and justify an efficient derivative pathway for $R(x)=x^{2} + 2$, then compute $R'(x)$.
Answer: $R'(x)=2 x$
Solution / scoring notes: Simplify first to $x^{2} + 2$; quotient rule is valid but unnecessarily long.
U3-SUM-V1-Q09
Section 3.3 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

If $x^2+y^2=25$, which expression is $dy/dx$?
  1. $-y/x$
  2. $-x/y$
  3. $x/y$
  4. $y/x$
Answer: B. $-x/y$
Solution / scoring notes: Implicit differentiation gives $2x+2y(dy/dx)=0$.
U3-SUM-V1-Q10
Section 3.3 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Find the tangent slope to $x^2+y^2=25$ at $(-4,3)$.
Answer: $\frac{4}{3}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $dy/dx=-x/y$ and substitute the point.
U3-SUM-V1-Q11
Section 3.3 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Differentiate implicitly and solve for $dy/dx$ if $xy+y^2=8$.
Answer: $-y/(x+2y)$
Solution / scoring notes: Product rule on $xy$ and chain factors on $y$ terms are both required.
U3-SUM-V1-Q12
Section 3.3 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

A student differentiates $x^2+xy+y^2=9$ as $2x+x+2y=0$. Critique the work, identify the missing dependency terms, and write the corrected derivative.
Answer: $dy/dx=-(2x+y)/(x+2y)$
Solution / scoring notes: The student omitted product-rule and $dy/dx$ factors.
U3-SUM-V1-Q13
Section 3.4 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

  1. $4$
  2. $\frac{1}{4}$
  3. $- \frac{1}{4}$
  4. $\frac{1}{2}$
Answer: B. $\frac{1}{4}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the reciprocal derivative at the corresponding input.
U3-SUM-V1-Q14
Section 3.4 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Let $f(x)=x^3+x$ and $g=f^{-1}$. Find $g'(-2)$.
Answer: $1/4$
Solution / scoring notes: $f(-1)=-2$ and $f'(-1)=4$, so the inverse derivative is reciprocal.
U3-SUM-V1-Q15
Section 3.4 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Differentiate $- \operatorname{asin}{\left(3 x - 1 \right)}$.
  1. $\frac{3 \left(3 x - 1\right)}{\sqrt{1 - \left(3 x - 1\right)^{2}}}$
  2. $\frac{1}{\sqrt{1 - \left(1 - 3 x\right)^{2}}}$
  3. $\frac{3}{\sqrt{1 - \left(3 x - 1\right)^{2}}}$
  4. $- \frac{3}{\sqrt{1 - \left(3 x - 1\right)^{2}}}$
Answer: D. $- \frac{3}{\sqrt{1 - \left(3 x - 1\right)^{2}}}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the inverse-sine derivative and the chain factor.
U3-SUM-V1-Q16
Section 3.4 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Explain why $f^{-1}$ does not mean $1/f$, then state the inverse-derivative formula in corresponding-value form.
Answer: $(f^{-1})'(a)=1/f'(f^{-1}(a))$
Solution / scoring notes: The inverse is a composition inverse; the derivative formula uses the original derivative at the matching inverse input.
U3-SUM-V1-Q17
Section 3.5 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Find $f''(x)$ for $f(x)=x^{4} + \sin{\left(2 x \right)}$.
  1. $4 x^{3} + 2 \cos{\left(2 x \right)}$

  3. $- 12 x^{2} + 4 \sin{\left(2 x \right)}$
  4. $12 x^{2} - 4 \sin{\left(2 x \right)}$
Answer: D. $12 x^{2} - 4 \sin{\left(2 x \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate twice.
U3-SUM-V1-Q18
Section 3.5 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

A particle has $s(t)=t^{3} - 3 t^{2} + 2 t$. Find velocity and acceleration at $t=2$.
Answer: $v(2)=2$, $a(2)=6$
Solution / scoring notes: First and second derivatives give velocity and acceleration.
U3-SUM-V1-Q19
Section 3.5 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

For $H(x)=(x^2+1)^3e^{2x}$, describe the derivative rule sequence before computing. Then find $H'(x)$.
Answer: $H'(x)=2 \left(x^{2} + 1\right)^{2} \left(x^{2} + 3 x + 1\right) e^{2 x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Product rule is outermost; chain rule is required in both differentiated factors.
U3-SUM-V1-Q20
Section 3.5 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 3 Summative - Version 1

Compare two plausible approaches to $R(x)=\frac{x(x^2+1)^2}{x}$. Choose the more efficient approach, compute $R'(x)$, and justify the strategy choice.
Answer: $R'(x)=4 x \left(x^{2} + 1\right)$
Solution / scoring notes: Cancel the common factor on the domain before differentiating; then $R'(x)=4 x \left(x^{2} + 1\right)$.

  1. $\left(2 x - 2\right) \sin{\left(3 x + 3 \right)} + \left(3 x^{2} - 6 x + 9\right) \cos{\left(3 x + 3 \right)}$
  2. $\left(2 - 2 x\right) \sin{\left(3 x + 3 \right)} + \left(- 3 x^{2} + 6 x - 9\right) \cos{\left(3 x + 3 \right)}$
  3. $\left(- x^{2} + 2 x - 3\right) \sin{\left(3 x + 3 \right)}$
  4. $\left(2 - 2 x\right) \sin{\left(3 x + 3 \right)} + \left(- 3 x^{2} + 6 x - 9\right) \cos{\left(3 x + 3 \right)} + 1$
Answer: B. $\left(2 - 2 x\right) \sin{\left(3 x + 3 \right)} + \left(- 3 x^{2} + 6 x - 9\right) \cos{\left(3 x + 3 \right)}$
Solution / scoring notes: Coordinate the outer product/quotient rule with inner chain factors.

Answer: $dy/dx=-(2x+y)/(x+2y)$

For $H(x)=(x^2+1)^3e^{2x}$, describe the derivative rule sequence before computing. Then find $H'(x)$.

State the result in exact form when an exact value is available. Focus the response on the strategy sequence requested here.

Answer: $H'(x)=2 \left(x^{2} + 1\right)^{2} \left(x^{2} + 3 x + 1\right) e^{2 x}$
Solution / scoring notes: Product rule is outermost; chain rule is required in both differentiated factors.

Compare two plausible approaches to $R(x)=\frac{x(x^2+1)^2}{x}$. Choose the more efficient approach, compute $R'(x)$, and justify the strategy choice.

Make the mathematical conclusion visible without relying on a cross-reference. Focus the response on the synthesis strategy requested here.

Answer: $R'(x)=4 x \left(x^{2} + 1\right)$
Solution / scoring notes: Cancel the common factor on the domain before differentiating; then $R'(x)=4 x \left(x^{2} + 1\right)$.
